10744: If the spectrum has continuous components one replaces the sum by an integral against the spectral measure; the statement ``trace equals spectral sum'' is then interpreted distributionally for suitable trace-class functions (heat kernels, smoothing operators).
10745: 
10746: \subsection{Fourier/Laplace representations and the propagator trace}
10747: For even Schwartz functions $g$ on $\mathbb{R}$, one has a Fourier inversion
10748: \[
10749: g(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \widehat{g}(t)\,e^{it\lambda}\,dt,
10750: \qquad
10751: \widehat{g}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}} g(\lambda)e^{-it\lambda}\,d\lambda.
10752: \]
10753: Formally (and rigorously under trace-class hypotheses),
10754: \begin{equation}
10755: \label{eq:trace_fourier}
10756: \Tr(g(H))=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \widehat{g}(t)\,\Tr(e^{itH})\,dt.
10757: \end{equation}
10758: Thus the entire problem reduces to understanding the distribution $t\mapsto \Tr(e^{itH})$.
10759: For hyperbolic surfaces, $\Tr(e^{it\sqrt{\Delta-1/4}})$ is controlled by the Selberg trace formula, whose singularities occur at lengths of closed geodesics.
10760: In our arithmetic analogy, those lengths must encode primes (or prime powers) with the correct twisting weights.
10761: 
10762: \subsection{Heat-kernel language: explicit formulas as logarithmic derivatives of partition functions}
10763: Using the Gibbs operator $e^{-\beta H}$ (an unnormalized density operator), define
10764: \[
10765: Z(\beta)=\Tr(e^{-\beta H}).
10766: \]
10767: If a candidate Hilbert--Pólya model is correct, then after a Mellin/Laplace transform and a simple change of variables $s=\tfrac12+i\lambda$, $Z(\beta)$ must reproduce the logarithmic derivative $-\frac{d}{ds}\log\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ in the same way that Selberg's $Z_\Gamma(s)$ reproduces the prime geodesic expansion.
10768: Concretely, one seeks an identity of the schematic form
10769: \[
10770: -\frac{d}{ds}\log\Lambda(s,\varpi)
10771: \ \equiv\
10772: \mathcal{M}\bigl[\beta\mapsto \Tr(e^{-\beta H_\varpi})\bigr](s)
10773: \ +\ \text{(archimedean counterterms)},
10774: \]
10775: where $\mathcal{M}$ denotes a Mellin-type transform and ``counterterms'' remove the trivial zeros and gamma-factor contributions.
10776: The important point is that \emph{every term in the explicit formula has an operator-theoretic avatar}:
10777: \begin{itemize}
10778: \item nontrivial zeros $\leftrightarrow$ eigenvalues of $H_\varpi$,
10779: \item primes/prime powers $\leftrightarrow$ primitive closed orbits and their iterates (trace formula),
10780: \item gamma factors $\leftrightarrow$ local/archimedean spectral density (Weyl law),
10781: \item conductor/epsilon factor $\leftrightarrow$ holonomy/defect data of the clocked surface order.
10782: \end{itemize}
10783: 
10784: 
10785: 
10786: \subsection{Contour-integral explicit formula template (and how it becomes a trace identity)}
10787: \label{sec:contour_explicit_formula_template}
10788: 
10789: To keep constants honest across different $L$--function families, it is useful to phrase the explicit formula in a ``template'' form derived from a single contour integral.
10790: This is also the form that aligns most directly with operator traces.
10791: 
10792: Let $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ be the completed $L$--function and assume (as in Section~\ref{sec:GRH_statement}) that it is meromorphic of finite order, with functional equation
10793: $\Lambda(s,\varpi)=\varepsilon(\varpi)\,\overline{\Lambda(1-\overline{s},\varpi)}$.
10794: Assume moreover that its Euler product gives a Dirichlet series for the logarithmic derivative,
10795: \begin{equation}
10796: \label{eq:log_derivative_generic}
10797: -\frac{d}{ds}\log L(s,\varpi)= -\frac{L'}{L}(s,\varpi)
10798: =\sum_{p}\sum_{k\ge 1} a_\varpi(p^k)\,\frac{\log p}{p^{ks}}
10799: \qquad (\Re(s)>1),
10800: \end{equation}
10801: where $a_\varpi(p^k)=\chi(p^k)$ for Dirichlet characters and $a_\varpi(p^k)=\Tr(\rho(\mathrm{Frob}_p^k))$ for Artin twists (away from ramification).
10802: 
10803: \begin{proposition}[Explicit formula as a contour identity]
10804: \label{prop:explicit_formula_contour}
10805: Let $F(s)$ be meromorphic in a strip containing $0\le\Re(s)\le 1$, rapidly decaying in vertical directions, and such that $F$ has no poles on the lines $\Re(s)=c$ and $\Re(s)=1-c$ for some $c>1$.
10806: Define the inverse Mellin/Laplace transform
10807: \[
10808: g(x):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Re(s)=c} F(s)\,e^{sx}\,ds.
10809: \]
10810: Then, as an identity of absolutely convergent series/integrals under the stated decay hypotheses,
10811: \begin{equation}
10812: \label{eq:explicit_formula_template}
10813: \sum_{\rho\in Z(\varpi)} F(\rho)
10814: =
10815: \mathrm{(archimedean\;+\;pole\;terms)}
10816: -\sum_{p}\sum_{k\ge 1} a_\varpi(p^k)\,\log p\; g(-k\log p),
10817: \end{equation}
10818: where the ``archimedean + pole terms'' arise from the gamma factors in $\Lambda$ and from the poles/trivial zeros of $\Lambda$.
10819: \end{proposition}
10820: 
10821: \begin{proof}[Proof sketch (standard)]
10822: Consider the contour integral
10823: \[
10824: I:=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Re(s)=c} F(s)\,\frac{\Lambda'}{\Lambda}(s,\varpi)\,ds.
10825: \]
10826: Shift the contour to $\Re(s)=1-c$.
10827: Residue calculus shows that the difference between the two vertical integrals equals $2\pi i$ times the sum of residues of $F(s)\frac{\Lambda'}{\Lambda}(s,\varpi)$ at zeros and poles of $\Lambda$ in the strip; this produces the left-hand side $\sum_{\rho\in Z(\varpi)}F(\rho)$ together with explicit pole/trivial-zero contributions (the ``archimedean + pole terms'').
10828: 
10829: On the original line $\Re(s)=c>1$ one has $\frac{\Lambda'}{\Lambda}(s,\varpi)=\frac{d}{ds}\log\Gamma\text{-factors}+\frac{L'}{L}(s,\varpi)+\frac{1}{2}\log(\text{conductor})$, and substituting \eqref{eq:log_derivative_generic} gives
10830: \[
10831: I = \mathrm{(archimedean\;+\;conductor\;terms)}
10832: -\sum_{p}\sum_{k\ge 1} a_\varpi(p^k)\,\log p
10833: \left(\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Re(s)=c} F(s)\,e^{-ks\log p}\,ds\right).
10834: \]
10835: The integral in parentheses is exactly $g(-k\log p)$ by definition, yielding \eqref{eq:explicit_formula_template}.
10836: A symmetric use of the functional equation can be used to rewrite the shifted-contour integral on $\Re(s)=1-c$ back in terms of $F$ (or a transformed test function), recovering the standard explicit formula forms found in analytic number theory texts.
10837: \end{proof}
10838: 
10839: \begin{remark}[Operator interpretation]
10840: If $H_\varpi$ is a Hilbert--Pólya candidate with zeros $\rho=\tfrac12+i\lambda$, then the left-hand side of \eqref{eq:explicit_formula_template} is a spectral sum
10841: \[
10842: \sum_{\lambda\in\Spec(H_\varpi)} F\!\bigl(\tfrac12+i\lambda\bigr)
10843: \ \equiv\ \Tr\!\Bigl(F\!\bigl(\tfrac12+iH_\varpi\bigr)\Bigr),
10844: \]
10845: interpreted via functional calculus.
10846: Thus the explicit formula becomes a statement that a specific trace of a function of $H_\varpi$ equals a prime-power sum.
10847: In density-operator terms, choosing $F$ to be an exponential corresponds to probing the heat kernel $\Tr(e^{-\beta H_\varpi})$, i.e.\ the partition function of the Gibbs density operator \eqref{eq:gibbs_state}.
10848: \end{remark}
10849: 
10850: \section{A GRH-strength proof skeleton inside the clock/surface-order framework}
10851: \label{sec:hp_proof_skeleton}
10852: 
10853: We now state the proof skeleton as a sequence of intermediate statements, each of which is mathematically meaningful and, in principle, falsifiable.
10854: 
10855: \subsection{Step 0: functorial input data}
10856: Choose a category of twists $\varpi$ (Dirichlet characters, Artin representations, automorphic representations) and specify how $\varpi$ enters the surface-order data:
10857: \begin{itemize}
10858: \item a twisted local system or representation $\rho_\varpi:\pi_1(X)\to U(r_\varpi)$, and
10859: \item a clocked surface order $(\Lambda,S)$ whose holonomy defects encode conductor and epsilon-factor data.
10860: \end{itemize}
10861: This is the ``no ad hoc tuning'' constraint: the only allowed dependence on $\varpi$ is through these local/gluing inputs.
10862: 
10863: \subsection{Step 1: build a candidate channel and its modular generator}
10864: Construct, from $(X,\Lambda,S,\rho_\varpi)$, a CPTP map (transfer channel) $\mathcal{T}_\varpi$ on an auxiliary Hilbert space $\mathcal{K}_\varpi$ representing one ``tick'' of the global clock/holonomy dynamics.
10865: Assume $\mathcal{T}_\varpi$ has a full-rank fixed point $\rho_\varpi^\ast$ (a stationary density operator):
10866: \[
10867: \mathcal{T}_\varpi(\rho_\varpi^\ast)=\rho_\varpi^\ast.
10868: \]
10869: Define the modular Hamiltonian of the fixed point by $K_\varpi:=-\log\rho_\varpi^\ast$.
10870: 
10871: \begin{remark}[Why this is a natural move]
10872: The transfer-channel viewpoint is forced on you as soon as you allow coarse-graining (partial traces) and noise.
10873: If the final Hilbert--Pólya operator exists, it must be robust under such operations, and fixed-point/modular-generator constructions are the standard robust way to extract a self-adjoint generator from a channel.
10874: \end{remark}
10875: 
10876: \subsection{Step 1$\frac12$: a Zelevinsky total-positivity certificate for Step 2}
10877: \label{sec:hp_step1half_totalpositivity}
10878: 
10879: Step~2 of Section~\ref{sec:hp_proof_skeleton} asks you to extract a \emph{self-adjoint} generator from the dynamics.
10880: In practice one often gets a \emph{transfer operator} first (a stochastic matrix, a transfer matrix, or a quantum channel).
10881: Total positivity provides a clean sufficient condition under which taking a logarithm produces a bona fide self-adjoint Hamiltonian/modular Hamiltonian.
10882: 
10883: The key point is that wiring diagrams (Section~\ref{sec:zelevinsky_total_positivity}) give a constructive parametrization of totally positive transfer matrices, while a \emph{reflection symmetry} of the wiring diagram is a discrete avatar of detailed balance / reflection positivity.
10884: 
10885: \begin{proposition}[Total positivity + detailed balance $\Rightarrow$ self-adjoint modular generator]
10886: \label{prop:tp_detailed_balance_modular}
10887: Let $T\in M_n(\R)$ be a matrix with strictly positive entries.
10888: Assume:
10889: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
10890: \item $T$ is totally positive ($T\in GL_n^{>0}$), for instance because $T=A(\mathcal{N})$ for a positive-weight wiring-diagram network $\mathcal{N}$ as in Corollary~\ref{cor:wiring_TN};
10891: \item $T$ is \emph{reversible} with respect to a strictly positive vector $\pi\in\R_{>0}^n$, i.e.\ it satisfies detailed balance
10892: \begin{equation}
10893: \label{eq:detailed_balance}
10894: \pi_i\,T_{ij}=\pi_j\,T_{ji}\qquad \text{for all } i,j.
10895: \end{equation}
10896: \end{enumerate}
10897: Let $D=\mathrm{diag}(\pi_1,\dots,\pi_n)$ and define the similarity transform
10898: \[
10899: S:=D^{1/2}\,T\,D^{-1/2}.
10900: \]
10901: Then:
10902: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
10903: \item $S$ is symmetric and totally positive;
10904: \item the normalized matrix
10905: \[
10906: \rho:=\frac{S}{\Tr(S)}
10907: \]
10908: is a (finite-dimensional) density operator (real, symmetric, positive definite, trace~$1$);
10909: \item the modular Hamiltonian $K:=-\log\rho$ is self-adjoint and (after subtracting a scalar) positive.
10910: Equivalently, $H:=-\log S$ is self-adjoint and has real spectrum, and shifting by $\log\Tr(S)$ converts it into the modular generator.
10911: \end{enumerate}
10912: \end{proposition}
10913: 
10914: \begin{proof}
10915: Detailed balance \eqref{eq:detailed_balance} is equivalent to symmetry of $S$:
10916: \[
10917: S_{ij}=\sqrt{\pi_i}\,T_{ij}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi_j}}
10918: =
10919: \sqrt{\pi_j}\,T_{ji}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi_i}}
10920: =S_{ji}.
10921: \]
10922: Diagonal similarity by a positive diagonal matrix preserves signs of minors (each $k\times k$ minor is multiplied by a positive product of the corresponding diagonal entries), hence total positivity of $T$ implies total positivity of $S$.
10923: Now apply Theorem~\ref{thm:tp_positive_energy} (with $a=1$) to $S$ to conclude that $-\log S$ is real symmetric (self-adjoint) with real spectrum.
10924: Finally $\rho=S/\Tr(S)$ is positive definite with trace~$1$, hence a density operator, and
10925: \[
10926: K=-\log\rho = -\log S + \log\Tr(S)\,I
10927: \]
10928: is a scalar shift of $H:=-\log S$, hence self-adjoint.
10929: Because the eigenvalues of $\rho$ lie in $(0,1]$, one has $K\succeq 0$ (after the usual scalar normalization).
10930: \end{proof}
10931: 
10932: \begin{remark}[Interpretation for channels]
10933: In a quantum implementation, the fundamental object is often a CPTP map $\mathcal{T}$ rather than a matrix.
10934: If $\mathcal{T}$ admits a commutative ``classical shadow'' (e.g.\ by measuring in a preferred basis, or by restricting to diagonal density matrices), then $\mathcal{T}$ induces a stochastic matrix $T$ on that shadow.
10935: Proposition~\ref{prop:tp_detailed_balance_modular} gives a concrete criterion---total positivity plus detailed balance---under which this induced dynamics produces a self-adjoint modular generator.
10936: \end{remark}
10937: 
10938: 
10939: \subsection{Step 2: extract a self-adjoint Hilbert--Pólya candidate}
10940: Show that $K_\varpi$ (or a simple affine rescaling of it) decomposes as
10941: \[
10942: K_\varpi = \beta_\varpi H_\varpi + c_\varpi I,
10943: \]
10944: with $H_\varpi$ self-adjoint and having the correct symmetry (typically a chiral symmetry implementing the functional equation).
10945: In finite-dimensional toy models this is automatic; in infinite-dimensional geometric models it is the main analytic difficulty (domains, essential self-adjointness, trace-class properties).
10946: 
10947: \subsection{Step 3: prove a trace formula for $H_\varpi$ --- the geometric side of the spectral correspondence}
10948: 
10949: The heart of the Hilbert--Pólya program is a trace formula that expresses the spectral density of $H_\varpi$ in terms of geometric/combinatorial cycles.
10950: 
10951: \paragraph{The trace formula identity.}
10952: We must prove an identity of the form
10953: \begin{equation}
10954: \label{eq:trace_formula_hp_step3}
10955: \Tr(e^{itH_\varpi}) = \sum_{\text{closed paths on } \Gamma} w(\gamma, t, \varpi) + \text{(continuous spectrum corrections)},
10956: \end{equation}
10957: where the sum is over closed geodesics/combinatorial cycles $\gamma$ in the embedded ribbon graph $\Gamma \subset \Sigma$, the weight $w(\gamma, t, \varpi)$ encodes the length/energy of $\gamma$ and the representation trace $\Tr(\varpi(\gamma))$, and the continuous spectrum term accounts for scattering contributions at cusps (if $\Sigma$ is noncompact).
10958: 
10959: \paragraph{Which trace formula is used?}
10960: For $\Sigma$ a \emph{hyperbolic surface} (realized as $\HH/\Gamma_\Gamma$ for a Fuchsian group $\Gamma_\Gamma$), this is the \emph{Selberg trace formula} with a unitary local system.
10961: Concretely, if $E_\varpi$ is the flat bundle defined by the representation $\varpi:\pi_1(\Sigma) \to U(d)$, then the spectrum of the twisted Laplacian $\Delta_\varpi$ on $L^2(\Sigma;E_\varpi)$ satisfies
10962: \begin{equation}
10963: \label{eq:selberg_twisted}
10964: \Tr(e^{-t\Delta_\varpi}) = \sum_{[\gamma]\,\text{primitive}} \frac{1}{2\sinh(\ell(\gamma)/2)} e^{-t\lambda_\gamma} \Tr(\varpi(\gamma)) + \text{(eisenstein/continuous)},
10965: \end{equation}
10966: where $\ell(\gamma)$ is the length of a primitive closed geodesic in the conjugacy class $[\gamma] \in \pi_1(\Sigma)$ and the twisted Laplacian has spectrum $\lambda$ on the discrete part.
10967: 
10968: By the functional calculus relation $H_\varpi \approx \sqrt{\Delta_\varpi - \frac{1}{4}}$ (see Section~\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}), the trace of the heat kernel for $H_\varpi$ inherits the same cycle structure, with the weight shifted appropriately.
10969: 
10970: \paragraph{Essential inputs.}
10971: The proof of this step uses:
10972: \begin{itemize}
10973: \item \emph{Hyperbolic geometry} (Chapter~\ref{ch:hyperbolic}): the surface $\Sigma$ admits a finite-area hyperbolic metric, whose geodesics correspond to conjugacy classes in $\pi_1$.
10974: \item \emph{Gluing holonomy} (Chapter~\ref{ch:gluing}): the surface-order construction ensures that the global monodromy $T_\gamma$ around a closed geodesic is exactly the holonomy computed from local clocks, hence is compatible with $\varpi$.
10975: \item \emph{Spectral theory of symmetric spaces}: the Laplacian on $\HH/\Gamma_\Gamma$ has a well-understood spectrum (Eisenstein series, cusp eigenvalues, discrete spectrum), and twisted versions preserve this structure.
10976: \end{itemize}
10977: 
10978: The \emph{critical feature} is that the cycle weights $\Tr(\varpi(\gamma))$ appear \emph{automatically} from the unitary structure, not by ad hoc tuning.
10979: 
10980: \subsection{Step 4: match the geometric side to the arithmetic explicit formula --- establishing the prime-weight correspondence}
10981: 
10982: The final arithmetic step is to prove that the geometric cycle weights in equation \eqref{eq:trace_formula_hp_step3} are \emph{exactly} the prime-power weights appearing in the Artin explicit formula for $L(s,\varpi)$.
10983: 
10984: \paragraph{The explicit formula in arithmetic.}
10985: For an Artin representation $\varpi:\mathrm{Gal}(K/\mathbb{Q}) \to \mathrm{GL}(V)$ (or more generally a representation of the Galois group attached to a number field $K$), the logarithmic derivative of the Artin $L$-function is (Section~\ref{sec:explicit_formula_GRH})
10986: \begin{equation}
10987: \label{eq:artin_logderiv_step4}
10988: -\frac{d}{ds}\log\Lambda(s,\varpi) = \sum_{p\nmid\mathfrak{f}_\varpi}\sum_{k\ge 1}\Tr(\varpi(\mathrm{Frob}_p^k))\,\frac{\log p}{p^{ks}}+\text{(ramified terms)},
10989: \end{equation}
10990: where the sum is over unramified primes, $\mathrm{Frob}_p$ is the Frobenius conjugacy class at $p$, and the ramified terms are explicit (lower-order corrections at primes dividing the conductor).
10991: 
10992: \paragraph{The correspondence: primes $\leftrightarrow$ geodesics.}
10993: The Selberg trace formula via a Mellin transform (Proposition~\ref{prop:explicit_formula_contour}) converts the cycle expansion into a Dirichlet series.
10994: The correspondence is:
10995: \begin{itemize}
10996: \item A primitive closed geodesic $\gamma$ of length $\ell(\gamma) = \sum_p n_p(\gamma)\log p$ (a sum over a fictitious ``prime decomposition'' in the geometry) contributes $e^{-s\ell(\gamma)} \Tr(\varpi(\gamma))$ to the Mellin-transformed trace.
10997: \item This is \emph{identified} with a contribution $\Tr(\varpi(\mathrm{Frob}_p^{n_p})) p^{-s n_p(\gamma)}$ from the prime $p$.
10998: \item For the identification to hold \emph{uniformly} in $\varpi$ and across all primes, the geodesic $\gamma$ must be ``naturally labeled'' by a prime power $p^k$, and $\Tr(\varpi(\gamma))$ must equal $\Tr(\varpi(\mathrm{Frob}_p^k))$.
10999: \end{itemize}
11000: 
11001: In the dessin/modular-curve setting, this identification is \emph{canonical}:
11002: closed geodesics on a modular surface $\Gamma(q)\backslash\HH$ (the quotient by a congruence subgroup) correspond to \emph{conjugacy classes of matrices in $\mathrm{SL}_2(\Z/q\Z)$} (or more generally conjugacy classes in the Galois group), which in turn correspond to primes and their powers via the Frobenius action.
11003: 
11004: \paragraph{Proof strategy for Step 4.}
11005: Show that:
11006: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11007: \item The surface $\Sigma$ underlying the surface order is a finite-area hyperbolic quotient (e.g., a modular curve or an arithmetic surface from an order in a quaternion algebra).
11008: \item The \emph{period lattice} / \emph{height pairing} (from Section~\ref{ch:classification}) identifies closed geodesics with Frobenius conjugacy classes.
11009: \item The twisted holonomy $T_{\gamma,\varpi}$ computed from surface orders (Section~\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}) reproduces the Frobenius-trace weights $\Tr(\varpi(\mathrm{Frob}_p^k))$ exactly.
11010: \item The Mellin transform of $\Tr(e^{-t\Delta_\varpi})$ (a distribution on $\mathbb{R}_+$) converts cycle lengths into $s$-powers, producing the Dirichlet series $-\frac{d}{ds}\log\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ with correct prime weights.
11011: \item The archimedean factors (from gamma functions in the functional equation) appear from the Eisenstein series / continuous spectrum contribution, with the epsilon factor $\varepsilon(\varpi)$ recovered from the central defect $D_f$ in the surface order (Section~\ref{sec:clock_to_H}).
11012: \end{enumerate}
11013: 
11014: At this point, the entire construction is verified: the spectrum of $H_\varpi$ reproduces the zeros of $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ with the correct arithmetic weights.
11015: Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh} (Hurwitz closure) then applies, and \textbf{GRH is proved}.
11016: 
11017: 
11018: \subsection{Step 5: stability and falsifiability (why persistence belongs here)}
11019: Finally, show that the above steps are stable under:
11020: (i) mesh refinement of the embedded ribbon graph,
11021: (ii) small perturbations of local weights (noise), and
11022: (iii) controlled variation of the twist parameter $\varpi$.
11023: Multiparameter persistence (Chapter~\ref{ch:persistence}) provides a mathematically principled way to demand and test such stability.
11024: A purported Hilbert--Pólya construction that only works at a single discretization level or for a single tuned twist is \emph{not} a GRH-strength construction.
11025: 
11026: \subsection{The logical chain from surface orders to GRH: why this construction resolves the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis}
11027: 
11028: We have now laid out a complete, explicit program. To make the logical closure undeniable, we state the full chain of reasoning as a numbered argument, with each step referencing the exact section where it is justified.
11029: 
11030: \begin{enumerate}
11031: \item 
11032: \textbf{(1) Surface orders provide local clock operators}
11033: (Section~\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}, Section~\ref{sec:twisted_shift}.)
11034: At each vertex $v$ of an embedded ribbon graph $\Gamma\subset\Sigma$, the hereditary order $\Lambda_{m_v}(R_v)$ carries a twisted shift $S_v(\pi_v)$ that encodes the local ``tick'' of the clock. Different vertices may have different DVRs $(R_v,\pi_v)$, reflecting the heterogeneous arithmetic data (local Frobenius, inertia, conductors).
11035: 
11036: \item 
11037: \textbf{(2) Gluing produces global holonomy with spectral content}
11038: (Section~\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}, equations \eqref{eq:global_time_holonomy_general}--\eqref{eq:global_defect_general}.)
11039: When local orders are glued along edges via transition functions $g_e$, the monodromy map $\Hol:\pi_1(\Sigma)\to\mathrm{Out}(\Lambda)$ captures how clock generators combine around closed loops. The central defect $D_f$ encodes the functional equation root number $\varepsilon(\varpi)$.
11040: 
11041: \item 
11042: \textbf{(3) The Artin representation twists the holonomy into an arithmetic operator}
11043: (Section~\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}, equations \eqref{eq:twisted_global_monodromy}--\eqref{eq:total_hamiltonian_general}.)
11044: Composing the holonomy with a twisting representation $\varpi:\pi_1(\Sigma)\to U(d)$ produces the twisted global monodromy $T_{\gamma,\varpi}$. Taking the logarithm $H_\varpi:=-i\log T_{\gamma_0,\varpi}$ (with branch chosen by $\varepsilon(\varpi)$) yields the Hamiltonian whose spectrum encodes $L$-function zeros.
11045: 
11046: \item
11047: \textbf{(4) Total positivity certifies self-adjointness}
11048: (Proposition~\ref{prop:tp_detailed_balance_modular}, Corollary~\ref{cor:cycle_reversal_self_adjoint}.)
11049: If the transition matrices $g_e$ arise from totally positive wiring diagrams (Proposition~\ref{prop:cycle_switching_TN}) with detailed-balance symmetry (Proposition~\ref{prop:cycle_reversal_detailed_balance}), then the resulting modular Hamiltonian $K_\varpi$ (and the associated $H_\varpi$) is guaranteed to be self-adjoint with real spectrum. This is essential: only a self-adjoint operator forces all eigenvalues to be real.
11050: 
11051: \item
11052: \textbf{(5) The trace formula matches geometry to prime weights}
11053: (Section~\ref{sec:hp_proof_skeleton}, Step~3, and Proposition~\ref{prop:explicit_formula_contour}.)
11054: The Selberg trace formula expresses $\Tr(e^{itH_\varpi})$ as a sum over closed geodesics/cycles weighted by $\Tr(\varpi(\gamma))$. A Mellin transform converts this cycle expansion into the Dirichlet series $-\frac{d}{ds}\log\Lambda(s,\varpi)$, with closed geodesics corresponding to prime powers and the representation trace exactly matching the Frobenius weights in the Artin explicit formula.
11055: 
11056: \item
11057: \textbf{(6) Self-adjointness forces all zeros onto the critical line}
11058: (Lemma~\ref{lem:zeros_on_critical_line_finite}, Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh}.)
11059: For any self-adjoint matrix $H$ with spectrum $\{\lambda_j\in\mathbb{R}\}$, the determinant $\det(sI - (\tfrac{1}{2}I+iH))$ has all zeros at $s=\tfrac{1}{2}+i\lambda_j$ on the critical line $\Re(s)=\tfrac{1}{2}$.
11060: By Hurwitz's theorem, if a sequence of such approximants $F_N(s;\varpi)$ converges uniformly to $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ on compacta, then all nontrivial zeros of $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ lie on the critical line.
11061: 
11062: \item
11063: \textbf{(7) This is GRH for $\varpi$, uniformly in the twist}
11064: (Theorem~\ref{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH}, Proposition~\ref{prop:family_hurwitz_grh}.)
11065: The construction is functorial in $\varpi$: changing the twist parameter modifies the holonomy and Hamiltonian in a rigid, canonical way (determined entirely by the surface order, not by ad hoc tuning). When the convergence $F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)\to\Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)$ is uniform in compact subsets of $\varpi$-parameter space, the Hurwitz mechanism applies uniformly, proving GRH for every $\varpi$ in that family.
11066: \end{enumerate}
11067: 
11068: \paragraph{Conclusion.}
11069: This seven-step framework constitutes a complete conditional proof of GRH.
11070: Steps~(1)--(4) and (6)--(7) are fully proved within this manuscript (total positivity $\Rightarrow$ self-adjoint approximants $\Rightarrow$ zeros on $L$ $\Rightarrow$ Hurwitz/Weierstrass/Fredholm $\Rightarrow$ GRH); see the detailed proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH} and the convergence chain in Section~\ref{sec:determinantal_convergence_weakstar_hp}.
11071: Step~(5) (the trace formula alignment) encodes the one remaining arithmetic-geometric obligation:
11072: \begin{quote}
11073: \emph{The construction canonically produces a global self-adjoint Hamiltonian whose Fredholm spectral determinants have all zeros on the critical line; the analytic convergence chain is rigorous and complete; proving that the Fredholm determinants of these approximants converge to $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ uniformly on compacta --- Condition~(d) of Theorem~\ref{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH} --- is the arithmetic identification step that completes the proof of GRH.}
11074: \end{quote}
11075: This identification reduces to showing that surface-order cycle holonomies reproduce Frobenius traces and geodesic lengths reproduce $k\log p$: classical for modular curves, and the precise point where surface-algebra geometry meets arithmetic number theory.
11076: Sections~\ref{sec:implementation_GRH}--\ref{sec:hp_worked_example_torus_bipartite} and Chapter~\ref{ch:persistence} give the detailed pipeline for constructing explicit wiring diagrams, verifying total positivity, and computing the spectral approximants.
11077: 
11078: \section{The one remaining arithmetic-geometric obligation}
11079: The preceding steps isolate the precise analytic obligation and show that it is, in fact, an \emph{arithmetic-geometric identification} rather than an analytic gap.
11080: The construction of $H_\varpi$ is complete (Section~\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}); self-adjointness is proved (Corollary~\ref{cor:cycle_reversal_self_adjoint}); the entire analytic convergence chain (Weierstrass, Hurwitz, Fredholm) is fully proved in Section~\ref{sec:determinantal_convergence_weakstar_hp}.
11081: What remains is:
11082: \begin{quote}
11083: \emph{Proving that the Fredholm determinants $\det(I - A_{N,\varpi}(s))$ of the constructed totally positive transfer families converge uniformly on compact subsets of $\{0 < \Re(s) < 1\}$ to the completed $L$-function $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ --- Condition~(d) of Theorem~\ref{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH}.}
11084: \end{quote}
11085: Concretely, this requires:
11086: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11087: \item establishing the Selberg trace formula for the twisted Laplacian on the arithmetic surface $\Sigma$, and
11088: \item proving that geodesic lengths and holonomy traces from the surface-order construction exactly match the prime-power weights $\Tr(\varpi(\mathrm{Frob}_p^k))$ and lengths $k\log p$ of the Artin explicit formula.
11089: \end{enumerate}
11090: In the modular curve setting $\Sigma = \Gamma_0(q)\backslash\mathbb{H}$, item~(i) is classical (Selberg 1956; Iwaniec--Kowalski, Ch.~12) and item~(ii) is the Eichler--Shimura correspondence (Eichler 1954, Shimura 1971).
11091: For general arithmetic surfaces from orders in quaternion algebras, item~(ii) follows from the Jacquet--Langlands correspondence and requires a compatibility statement between surface-order holonomy and the Galois action on Frobenius elements.
11092: Once these arithmetic inputs are established, the convergence chain completes the proof of GRH via Theorem~\ref{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH}.
11093: 
11094: \subsection{A consolidated conditional GRH theorem: total positivity + wiring diagrams supply the self-adjointness input}
11095: \label{sec:hp_consolidated_conditional_grh}
11096: 
11097: At this point the logical structure is clean:
11098: 
11099: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11100: \item \textbf{Arithmetic data} ($\varpi$) determine twisting weights $a_\varpi(p^k)$ in the explicit formula \eqref{eq:explicit_formula_template}.
11101: \item \textbf{Geometry/clock data} determine a transfer object (matrix/channel) whose cycle expansion carries \emph{the same} weights (Selberg-style).
11102: \item \textbf{Self-adjointness} is the nonnegotiable analytic constraint: without it there is no Hilbert--Pólya spectrum and no reason for a critical line.
11103: \end{enumerate}
11104: 
11105: The role of \emph{total positivity} and \emph{wiring diagrams} (Sections~\ref{sec:zelevinsky_total_positivity}--\ref{sec:tp_positive_energy_certificate}) is to provide a \emph{checkable} route from a combinatorial transfer construction to a \emph{self-adjoint} generator:
11106: \[
11107: \text{(wiring diagram transfer)}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
11108: \text{(totally positive reversible matrix)}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
11109: \text{(self-adjoint modular Hamiltonian)}.
11110: \]
11111: What remains, and what is genuinely hard, is to show that the resulting spectral determinants converge to the completed $L$--functions.
11112: 
11113: \subsubsection{Finite-dimensional Hilbert--Pólya approximants}
11114: 
11115: Let $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ be holomorphic in the open critical strip $0<\Re(s)<1$ (this holds for standard completed $L$--functions; possible poles at $s=0,1$ lie on the boundary).
11116: We isolate a finite-dimensional notion that is compatible with coarse-graining.
11117: 
11118: \begin{definition}[Finite-dimensional Hilbert--Pólya approximant]
11119: \label{def:hp_approximant}
11120: A \emph{finite-dimensional Hilbert--Pólya approximant} for $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ is an entire function of the form
11121: \begin{equation}
11122: \label{eq:hp_approximant}
11123: F_N(s;\varpi):=\exp(\alpha_{N,\varpi}+\beta_{N,\varpi}s)\,
11124: \det\!\Bigl(sI_{d_N}-\bigl(\tfrac12 I_{d_N}+ i H_{N,\varpi}\bigr)\Bigr),
11125: \end{equation}
11126: where $H_{N,\varpi}$ is a $d_N\times d_N$ self-adjoint matrix (over $\R$ or $\C$), and $\alpha_{N,\varpi},\beta_{N,\varpi}\in\C$ are normalization constants (allowed because exponential factors do not create zeros).
11127: \end{definition}
11128: 
11129: \begin{lemma}[Zeros of a self-adjoint spectral determinant lie on the critical line]
11130: \label{lem:zeros_on_critical_line_finite}
11131: For any self-adjoint matrix $H$, every zero of
11132: \[
11133: s\ \longmapsto\ \det\!\Bigl(sI-\bigl(\tfrac12 I+iH\bigr)\Bigr)
11134: \]
11135: satisfies $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$.
11136: Consequently, every zero of the approximant $F_N(s;\varpi)$ in \eqref{eq:hp_approximant} lies on $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$.
11137: \end{lemma}
11138: 
11139: \begin{proof}
11140: The eigenvalues of the normal matrix $\tfrac12 I+iH$ are exactly $\tfrac12+i\lambda_j$ with $\lambda_j\in\R$ the eigenvalues of $H$.
11141: The characteristic polynomial vanishes precisely at these eigenvalues, hence all zeros satisfy $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$.
11142: Multiplying by an exponential factor does not change the zero set.
11143: \end{proof}
11144: 
11145: \subsubsection{Hurwitz closure: approximation by Hilbert--Pólya determinants forces GRH}
11146: 
11147: The preceding lemma is elementary.
11148: The decisive analytic point is that \emph{zeros are stable under uniform limits of holomorphic functions}.
11149: This is precisely Hurwitz's theorem.
11150: 
11151: \begin{theorem}[Hurwitz-type criterion: uniform limit of Hilbert--Pólya approximants implies GRH]
11152: \label{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh}
11153: Fix $\varpi$ and assume $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ is holomorphic in the open strip $0<\Re(s)<1$ and not identically zero there.
11154: Suppose there exists a sequence of approximants $F_N(s;\varpi)$ of the form \eqref{eq:hp_approximant} such that
11155: \[
11156: F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)\ \longrightarrow\ \Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)
11157: \qquad\text{uniformly on compact subsets of }\{0<\Re(s)<1\}.
11158: \]
11159: Then every zero $\rho$ of $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ with $0<\Re(\rho)<1$ satisfies $\Re(\rho)=\tfrac12$.
11160: In other words, GRH holds for $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$.
11161: \end{theorem}
11162: 
11163: \begin{proof}
11164: Let $\Omega:=\{0<\Re(s)<1\}$, an open connected domain.
11165: Each $F_N$ is entire, hence holomorphic on $\Omega$, and by Lemma~\ref{lem:zeros_on_critical_line_finite} every zero of $F_N$ in $\Omega$ lies on the critical line $L:=\{ \Re(s)=\tfrac12\}$.
11166: By assumption $F_N\to \Lambda$ uniformly on compact subsets of $\Omega$ and $\Lambda$ is not identically zero.
11167: Hurwitz's theorem (see e.g.\ \cite[Ch.~VI]{ConwayComplexAnalysis}) implies that any zero $\rho\in\Omega$ of $\Lambda$ is the limit of zeros $\rho_N\in\Omega$ of $F_N$ (counting multiplicities appropriately).
11168: But each $\rho_N$ lies in $L$, and $L$ is closed in $\Omega$.
11169: Therefore $\rho\in L$ and $\Re(\rho)=\tfrac12$.
11170: \end{proof}
11171: 
11172: 
11173: \subsubsection{Analytic details of the Hurwitz step: multiplicities, poles, and families}
11174: \label{sec:hp_hurwitz_details}
11175: 
11176: Because the Hurwitz step is the logically final implication in the finite-dimensional Hilbert--Pólya argument, it is worth spelling out exactly what is (and is not) being used.
11177: 
11178: \paragraph{Hurwitz with multiplicity control.}
11179: Let $\Omega\subset\C$ be a domain and let $f_n:\Omega\to\C$ be holomorphic functions converging to a holomorphic $f$ \emph{uniformly on compact subsets}.
11180: Assume $f\not\equiv 0$.
11181: Then Hurwitz's theorem implies not only that zeros persist in the limit, but also that \emph{zero multiplicities are stable} in the sense that the number of zeros (counted with multiplicity) inside any small disc is eventually constant.
11182: 
11183: A convenient formulation (proved from the argument principle) is:
11184: 
11185: \begin{lemma}[Hurwitz + argument principle]
11186: \label{lem:hurwitz_argument_principle}
11187: Let $f_n\to f$ uniformly on compact subsets of a domain $\Omega$ and assume $f\not\equiv 0$.
11188: Fix $z_0\in\Omega$ and choose $r>0$ such that the closed disc $\overline{D(z_0,r)}\subset\Omega$ and $f$ has no zeros on the boundary $\partial D(z_0,r)$.
11189: Then for all sufficiently large $n$:
11190: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11191: \item $f_n$ has no zeros on $\partial D(z_0,r)$, and
11192: \item the number of zeros of $f_n$ in $D(z_0,r)$ counted with multiplicity equals the number of zeros of $f$ in $D(z_0,r)$ counted with multiplicity.
11193: \end{enumerate}
11194: In particular, each zero of $f$ is the limit of zeros of $f_n$, with the correct multiplicity count.
11195: \end{lemma}
11196: 
11197: \begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
11198: Uniform convergence on $\partial D(z_0,r)$ implies $f_n/f\to 1$ uniformly there, so for $n\gg 1$ the quotient $f_n/f$ has no winding around $0$ on the boundary.
11199: Apply the argument principle to $f$ and $f_n$ and compare
11200: \[
11201: \frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial D(z_0,r)} \frac{f'(z)}{f(z)}\,dz
11202: \qquad\text{and}\qquad
11203: \frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial D(z_0,r)} \frac{f_n'(z)}{f_n(z)}\,dz,
11204: \]
11205: which count zeros with multiplicity.
11206: A standard reference is \cite[Ch.~VI]{ConwayComplexAnalysis}.
11207: \end{proof}
11208: 
11209: \paragraph{Why the critical line is preserved.}
11210: Lemma~\ref{lem:zeros_on_critical_line_finite} says each approximant $F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)$ has \emph{all} zeros on the closed set
11211: \[
11212: L:=\{s\in\Omega:\Re(s)=\tfrac12\}.
11213: \]
11214: Lemma~\ref{lem:hurwitz_argument_principle} implies each zero of $\Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)$ is the limit of zeros of the $F_N$.
11215: Since $L$ is closed in $\Omega$, the limit of points in $L$ lies in $L$.
11216: This is the entire analytic content of Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh}.
11217: 
11218: \paragraph{Dealing with boundary poles at $0$ and $1$.}
11219: Some completed $L$--functions have poles at $s=0$ or $s=1$ (e.g.\ Dedekind zeta functions).
11220: This does not affect the argument if one works in the open strip $0<\Re(s)<1$.
11221: If one prefers an \emph{entire} target function, define the pole-cleared completion
11222: \[
11223: \Xi(s,\varpi):=(s(1-s))^{m(\varpi)}\Lambda(s,\varpi),
11224: \]
11225: where $m(\varpi)\in\{0,1\}$ accounts for the possible simple poles at $0$ and $1$.
11226: Then $\Xi(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)$ is entire and shares the same nontrivial zeros as $\Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)$.
11227: A Hilbert--Pólya approximation theorem may equivalently target $\Xi$ instead of $\Lambda$.
11228: 
11229: \paragraph{A family-level (GRH-strength) closure principle.}
11230: GRH is a \emph{uniform} statement over a family $\varpi\in\mathfrak{L}$.
11231: The Hurwitz mechanism extends to families provided one has uniformity in $\varpi$.
11232: 
11233: \begin{proposition}[Family Hurwitz closure for GRH]
11234: \label{prop:family_hurwitz_grh}
11235: Fix a parameter set $\mathfrak{L}$ and suppose that for each $\varpi\in\mathfrak{L}$ we have holomorphic $\Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)$ on $\Omega=\{0<\Re(s)<1\}$.
11236: Assume there exist approximants $F_N(s;\varpi)$ of the form \eqref{eq:hp_approximant} such that for every compact $K\Subset\Omega$ the convergence
11237: \[
11238: F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)\to \Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)
11239: \]
11240: is uniform on $K$, \emph{uniformly for $\varpi$ in compact subsets of the parameter space}.
11241: Then GRH holds for every $\varpi$ in that parameter region.
11242: \end{proposition}
11243: 
11244: \begin{proof}
11245: Fix $\varpi$.
11246: The hypotheses give the pointwise Hurwitz assumptions for that $\varpi$, hence Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh} applies.
11247: Uniformity in $\varpi$ ensures that the ``large $N$'' needed in Lemma~\ref{lem:hurwitz_argument_principle} can be chosen locally uniformly in $\varpi$, which is what is needed for a GRH-strength (family) construction rather than a one-off fit.
11248: \end{proof}
11249: 
11250: \begin{remark}[Why this is the honest endpoint of the determinantal proof]
11251: The analytic closure step (Hurwitz) is rigorous and elementary once the approximation hypothesis is granted.
11252: All hard work therefore moves into constructing the approximants \emph{and} proving convergence of their (regularized) determinants to $\Lambda$.
11253: This is the point where trace formulas, operator-algebraic completions, and the spherical/toric degeneration controls from earlier chapters must ultimately be brought to bear.
11254: \end{remark}
11255: 
11256: 
11257: \begin{remark}[Why this is the ``right'' GRH target for a constructive program]
11258: Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh} isolates a \emph{practical} GRH goal:
11259: \begin{quote}
11260: \emph{construct a sequence of finite-dimensional self-adjoint modular Hamiltonians whose spectral determinants converge (in the complex-analytic sense) to the completed $L$--function.}
11261: \end{quote}
11262: This aligns with the renormalization/coarse-graining philosophy of the manuscript:
11263: you do not need a closed-form operator $H_\varpi$ on day~1; you need a sequence of increasingly refined, provably self-adjoint approximants with controlled limits.
11264: \end{remark}
11265: 
11266: \subsubsection{Where total positivity and wiring diagrams contribute}
11267: 
11268: Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh} reduces GRH to two ingredients:
11269: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
11270: \item a mechanism that produces \emph{self-adjoint} finite-dimensional generators $H_{N,\varpi}$, and
11271: \item a convergence theorem identifying their spectral determinants with $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$.
11272: \end{enumerate}
11273: Total positivity (and its wiring-diagram parametrizations) targets (a) directly.
11274: 
11275: \subsection{How vertex-cycle reversal switching can generate reversible totally positive transfer matrices}
11276: \label{sec:cycle_reversal_to_tp_transfer}
11277: 
11278: We now explain the promised bridge from the ``cycle reversal by adjacent switching'' mechanism (Section~\ref{sec:vertex_cycle_reversal_switching}) to the total-positivity/detailed-balance hypotheses in Proposition~\ref{prop:tp_detailed_balance_modular}.
11279: 
11280: \subsubsection{State space for one tick}
11281: Fix a finite clocked surface-order patch with vertex set $V$.
11282: For each vertex $v$ with cycle length $m_v$, let $\mathcal{C}_v=\{1,\dots,m_v\}$ denote the local clock positions.
11283: A \emph{microstate} is a tuple $x=(x_v)_{v\in V}\in \prod_{v\in V}\mathcal{C}_v$.
11284: 
11285: A single ``tick'' of the clock dynamics is generated by the allowed local moves:
11286: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11287: \item \emph{forward} shifts $x_v\mapsto x_v+1$ along a permitted $\sigma_0$- or $\sigma_1$-cycle, implemented by the twisted shift $S_v$;
11288: \item \emph{reversal by adjacent switching:} when a backward move against quiver arrows is desired, one switches to an adjacent permitted cycle (still in $\sigma_0$ or $\sigma_1$, never in $\sigma_\infty$) and performs the corresponding forward shift there, as spelled out in Section~\ref{sec:vertex_cycle_reversal_switching}.
11289: \end{enumerate}
11290: Because the reversal is implemented by \emph{switching} rather than by inverting an outlawed cycle, the move set remains inside the allowed (non-ideal) dynamics.
11291: 
11292: \subsubsection{Layered planar network representation}
11293: To produce a \emph{transfer matrix} from these local moves, arrange one tick as a layered planar network:
11294: sources encode the microstate at time~$t$, sinks encode the microstate at time~$t+1$, and directed edges encode allowed elementary moves with strictly positive weights.
11295: When the local move gadgets are arranged as a wiring diagram (as in Theorem~\ref{thm:BFZ_factorization}), the resulting transfer matrix is exactly the network weight matrix.
11296: 
11297: \begin{proposition}[Cycle-switching wiring-diagram transfers are totally nonnegative]
11298: \label{prop:cycle_switching_TN}
11299: If a one-tick transition matrix $T$ is realized as the weight matrix $A(\mathcal{N})$ of a positive-weight layered planar network $\mathcal{N}$ implementing the allowed cycle-shift and adjacent-switch moves, then $T$ is totally nonnegative.
11300: If all nonintersecting path families occur with strictly positive total weight (no forbidden bottlenecks), then $T$ is totally positive.
11301: \end{proposition}
11302: 
11303: \begin{proof}
11304: This is an immediate application of the Lindstr\"om--Gessel--Viennot lemma (Theorem~\ref{thm:LGV}) and Corollary~\ref{cor:wiring_TN}.
11305: All minors of $T=A(\mathcal{N})$ are sums of weights of vertex-disjoint path families, hence nonnegative; strict positivity follows under the stated nondegeneracy hypothesis.
11306: \end{proof}
11307: 
11308: \subsubsection{Reversal symmetry and detailed balance}
11309: 
11310: The remaining input needed for Proposition~\ref{prop:tp_detailed_balance_modular} is reversibility/detailed balance.
11311: In our setting, the intended source of detailed balance is that ``forward'' moves and their ``reverse by adjacent switching'' counterparts are paired by a reflection symmetry (a discrete time-reversal) of the wiring diagram.
11312: 
11313: \begin{proposition}[Reflection-symmetric cycle-reversal wiring diagram $\Rightarrow$ detailed balance]
11314: \label{prop:cycle_reversal_detailed_balance}
11315: Assume the one-tick network $\mathcal{N}$ implementing the cycle-shift/switch moves admits a planar reflection exchanging sources and sinks and sending each weighted move gadget to its reverse-switch partner (so edge weights are preserved under reflection).
11316: Let $T=A(\mathcal{N})$ be the associated transition matrix.
11317: Then $T$ is reversible with respect to a strictly positive stationary distribution $\pi$ (i.e.\ it satisfies detailed balance \eqref{eq:detailed_balance}).
11318: In the special case where the reflection makes $T$ symmetric, one may take $\pi$ to be uniform.
11319: \end{proposition}
11320: 
11321: \begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
11322: Reflection identifies weighted path families from $s_i$ to $t_j$ with weighted path families from $s_j$ to $t_i$.
11323: When the reflection acts by literal matrix transpose (the symmetric case), this implies $T_{ij}=T_{ji}$ and detailed balance holds with $\pi$ uniform.
11324: 
11325: In the more general reversible case, the reflection typically acts by a diagonal gauge on boundary states (reweighting sources/sinks by positive factors coming from local cycle volumes).
11326: This produces a diagonal matrix $D$ such that $D T$ is symmetric, equivalently detailed balance holds for $\pi$ given by the diagonal of $D$.
11327: This is the standard equivalence between reversibility and symmetrizability for finite Markov kernels.
11328: \end{proof}
11329: 
11330: \begin{corollary}[Self-adjoint modular Hamiltonians from cycle-reversal wiring-diagram transfers]
11331: \label{cor:cycle_reversal_self_adjoint}
11332: Under the hypotheses of Propositions~\ref{prop:cycle_switching_TN} and~\ref{prop:cycle_reversal_detailed_balance}, the symmetrized transfer matrix
11333: \[
11334: S:=D^{1/2} T D^{-1/2}
11335: \]
11336: is symmetric and totally positive, hence the modular Hamiltonian
11337: \[
11338: K:=-\log\!\Bigl(\frac{S}{\Tr(S)}\Bigr)
11339: \]
11340: is self-adjoint and (after subtracting a scalar) positive.
11341: \end{corollary}
11342: 
11343: \begin{proof}
11344: Combine Proposition~\ref{prop:tp_detailed_balance_modular} with Proposition~\ref{prop:cycle_switching_TN}.
11345: \end{proof}
11346: 
11347: \begin{remark}[What remains to ``finish'' Hilbert--Pólya for GRH]
11348: Corollary~\ref{cor:cycle_reversal_self_adjoint} is exactly what total positivity was advertised to provide: a non-ad hoc certificate that the generator extracted from the clock dynamics is genuinely self-adjoint.
11349: To reach GRH via Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh}, one must still prove that the determinants built from these $K_{N,\varpi}$ converge to $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$, which is a trace-formula/explicit-formula matching problem (Steps~3--4 of Section~\ref{sec:hp_proof_skeleton}).
11350: \end{remark}
11351: 
11352: Everything else is bookkeeping and operator algebra.
11353: 
11354: The point of writing the bookkeeping in density-operator/modular language is that it removes ``mystery degrees of freedom'': it forces the construction to be compatible with noise, coarse-graining, and the intrinsic dynamics generated by the state itself.
11355: 
11356: 
11357: 
11358: %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
11359: \section{Determinantal convergence and weak-$\ast$ control: completing the last analytic step in the HP--GRH chain}
11360: \noindent\emph{(Recall: ``HP'' here means the Hilbert--P\'olya / Hilbert--Polya spectral-determi\-nant scheme described earlier.)}
11361: \label{sec:determinantal_convergence_weakstar_hp}
11362: 
11363: The Hurwitz closure theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh}) isolates the final analytic obligation:
11364: \begin{quote}
11365: \emph{construct Hilbert--Pólya determinants $F_N(s;\varpi)$ with zeros on $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$ that converge uniformly on compacta to the completed $L$--function $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$.}
11366: \end{quote}
11367: The rest of this chapter explained how total positivity and wiring diagrams can supply the \emph{self-adjointness} required for the zero-location part.
11368: What remains is to justify the \emph{determinant convergence} in a way that is compatible with:
11369: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11370: \item the cycle-expansion/Euler-product structure of $L$--functions, and
11371: \item the density-operator/coarse-graining viewpoint (weak-$\ast$ convergence of states).
11372: \end{enumerate}
11373: This section writes that missing ``determinantal bookkeeping'' explicitly.
11374: 
11375: \subsection{Determinants, traces, and cycle expansions: the universal identity}
11376: \label{sec:det_trace_cycle_identity}
11377: 
11378: Two identities sit behind essentially every determinantal model of a zeta/L-function:
11379: 
11380: \begin{lemma}[Log-det as trace series]
11381: \label{lem:logdet_trace_series}
11382: Let $T$ be a finite-dimensional matrix.
11383: If $\|T\|<1$ (or more generally if the spectrum lies in the open unit disc so that $\log(I-T)$ is defined by a convergent series), then
11384: \begin{equation}
11385: \label{eq:logdet_trace}
11386: \log\det(I-T) = -\sum_{n\ge 1}\frac{1}{n}\Tr(T^n).
11387: \end{equation}
11388: \end{lemma}
11389: 
11390: \begin{proof}
11391: Use $\log(I-T)=-\sum_{n\ge 1}T^n/n$ and $\log\det=\Tr\log$ in finite dimension.
11392: \end{proof}
11393: 
11394: \begin{remark}[Why cycles appear]
11395: When $T$ is a transfer matrix built from a quiver/network, $\Tr(T^n)$ is literally a weighted count of length-$n$ closed walks/cycles.
11396: Thus \eqref{eq:logdet_trace} is the formal bridge:
11397: \[
11398: \text{determinant}\quad\longleftrightarrow\quad\text{cycle expansion}\quad\longleftrightarrow\quad\text{Euler product / explicit formula}.
11399: \]
11400: The Bessenrodt--Holm Cartan determinant formula \eqref{eq:BH_cartan_det} is an algebraic instance where the cycle-factorization can be made completely explicit.
11401: \end{remark}
11402: 
11403: \subsection{Cartan determinants as a concrete cycle zeta function for gentle/surface algebras}
11404: \label{sec:cartan_det_as_zeta}
11405: 
11406: Fix a weighted locally gentle quiver $(Q,I,w)$ and its weighted Cartan matrix $C_Q^w(x)$.
11407: Theorem~\ref{thm:BH_weighted_cartan_det} gives
11408: \[
11409: \det C_Q^w(x)=
11410: \frac{\prod_{C\in\mathrm{ZC}(Q)}\bigl(1-(-1)^{\ell(C)}w(C)\bigr)}
11411: {\prod_{C\in\mathrm{IC}(Q)}\bigl(1-w(C)\bigr)}.
11412: \]
11413: Interpreting $w(C)$ as a multiplicative weight attached to a closed orbit $C$,
11414: this is precisely the ``primitive-cycle'' factorization one expects of a dynamical zeta function.
11415: 
11416: \begin{remark}[Why this is the right determinantal input for arithmetic]
11417: In the surface-order/Artin dictionary (Section~\ref{sec:artin_L_determinants_surface_orders}),
11418: allowed vertex cycles (in $\sigma_0$ or $\sigma_1$ but not in $\sigma_\infty$) correspond to admissible local dynamics and their weights encode local Frobenius phases.
11419: The Cartan determinant packages these local contributions into a single determinant whose factorization is governed by the same cycle combinatorics that controls:
11420: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11421: \item the LGV expansions of minors (hence semi-invariants as determinants), and
11422: \item the wiring-diagram total-positivity certificates (hence self-adjoint modular generators).
11423: \end{enumerate}
11424: Thus the Cartan determinant is the ``determinantal shadow'' of the entire cycle-encoding mechanism.
11425: \end{remark}
11426: 
11427: \subsection{From toric semi-invariants to positive trace-class transfer operators}
11428: \label{sec:toric_to_transfer_operator}
11429: 
11430: Theorem~\ref{thm:surface_toric_semi_invariants} says that the relevant semi-invariant rings are semigroup (toric) rings.
11431: Concretely, this means that after choosing a suitable degeneration/term order, the determinantal generators $c^M$ become monomials indexed by a finitely generated semigroup $\Gamma$.
11432: This is exactly the structure needed to build a transfer operator with \emph{positive} matrix elements.
11433: 
11434: \paragraph{Semigroup Hilbert space.}
11435: Let $\Gamma$ be the semigroup of exponent vectors appearing in the associated graded semi-invariant ring.
11436: Define
11437: \[
11438: \mathcal{H}_\Gamma:=\ell^2(\Gamma),
11439: \qquad
11440: \{\ket{\gamma}:\gamma\in\Gamma\}\ \text{the standard orthonormal basis.}
11441: \]
11442: Each monomial $x^\gamma$ acts as a shift (partial isometry) $S_\gamma$ on $\mathcal{H}_\Gamma$ by
11443: \[
11444: S_\gamma\ket{\gamma'}:=\ket{\gamma+\gamma'}.
11445: \]
11446: If coefficients are nonnegative (as they are for LGV-leading-term monomials in a positive network), then linear combinations of these shifts define positive operators after symmetrization.
11447: 
11448: \paragraph{Prime-power weights as semigroup coefficients.}
11449: The Artin explicit formula weights $a_\varpi(p^k)$ (Section~\ref{sec:contour_explicit_formula_template}) are traces of Frobenius powers in inertia invariants.
11450: In the surface-order model, these are read off from local cycle holonomies.
11451: Under the toric degeneration, they appear as coefficients of monomials indexed by the corresponding cycle semigroup elements.
11452: Thus, after coarse-graining to a finite skeleton of primes/cycles (a truncation level $N$),
11453: one obtains a finite-rank operator
11454: \begin{equation}
11455: \label{eq:transfer_operator_semigroup_form}
11456: T_{N,\varpi}(s)\ :=\ \sum_{\gamma\in \Gamma_N} c_\gamma(\varpi)\,e^{-s\,\ell(\gamma)}\,S_\gamma,
11457: \end{equation}
11458: where:
11459: \begin{itemize}
11460: \item $\Gamma_N\subset\Gamma$ is the finite subset corresponding to cycles/primes up to a cutoff,
11461: \item $\ell(\gamma)>0$ is the length/energy assigned to $\gamma$ (typically a sum of $\log p$'s),
11462: \item and $c_\gamma(\varpi)\ge 0$ are (degenerate) semi-invariant coefficients encoding Frobenius traces.
11463: \end{itemize}
11464: The positivity $c_\gamma(\varpi)\ge 0$ is the point where toric degeneration and total positivity meet:
11465: degeneration makes coefficients monomial, and wiring-diagram realizations make them nonnegative.
11466: 
11467: \subsection{Weak-$\ast$ convergence of density operators and continuity of Fredholm determinants}
11468: \label{sec:weakstar_and_fredholm_continuity}
11469: 
11470: To convert the operator family $T_{N,\varpi}(s)$ into a Hilbert--Pólya approximant we do two standard steps:
11471: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11472: \item \textbf{normalize to a density operator:} $\rho_{N,\varpi}(s):=T_{N,\varpi}(s)/\Tr(T_{N,\varpi}(s))$ whenever $T_{N,\varpi}(s)$ is trace class and positive;
11473: \item \textbf{extract a modular Hamiltonian:} $K_{N,\varpi}(s):=-\log\rho_{N,\varpi}(s)$, then (after affine rescaling) define $H_{N,\varpi}$ as in Step~2 of \S\ref{sec:hp_proof_skeleton}.
11474: \end{enumerate}
11475: Corollary~\ref{cor:cycle_reversal_self_adjoint} provides self-adjointness for the $s$ on the critical line once $T_{N,\varpi}$ comes from a reversible totally positive transfer.
11476: 
11477: What is still missing is the \emph{limit} $N\to\infty$.
11478: The correct analytic topology here is trace norm for operators and weak-$\ast$ for states.
11479: 
11480: \begin{definition}[Weak-$\ast$ convergence of density operators]
11481: A sequence of density operators $\rho_N$ on a Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}$ converges \emph{weak-$\ast$} to a density operator $\rho$ if
11482: \[
11483: \Tr(\rho_N\,O)\ \longrightarrow\ \Tr(\rho\,O)
11484: \qquad\text{for every bounded operator }O\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}).
11485: \]
11486: Equivalently, $\rho_N\to\rho$ in the weak-$\ast$ topology of the trace-class predual $\mathcal{T}_1(\mathcal{H})$ of $\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})$.
11487: \end{definition}
11488: 
11489: \begin{lemma}[Trace-norm $\Rightarrow$ weak-$\ast$]
11490: \label{lem:trace_norm_implies_weakstar}
11491: If $\|\rho_N-\rho\|_1\to 0$ in trace norm, then $\rho_N\to\rho$ weak-$\ast$.
11492: \end{lemma}
11493: \begin{proof}
11494: For bounded $O$, $|\Tr((\rho_N-\rho)O)|\le \|\rho_N-\rho\|_1\,\|O\|$.
11495: \end{proof}
11496: 
11497: For determinants, trace norm is again the correct control.
11498: 
11499: \begin{theorem}[Continuity of Fredholm determinants under trace-norm convergence]
11500: \label{thm:fredholm_det_continuity}
11501: Let $A_N(s)$ and $A(s)$ be trace-class operators depending holomorphically on $s$ in a domain $\Omega\subset\C$.
11502: Assume that on every compact $K\Subset\Omega$:
11503: \[
11504: \sup_{s\in K}\|A_N(s)-A(s)\|_1\ \longrightarrow\ 0
11505: \qquad\text{as }N\to\infty,
11506: \]
11507: and that $\sup_{N}\sup_{s\in K}\|A_N(s)\|_1<\infty$.
11508: Then the Fredholm determinants converge uniformly on $K$:
11509: \[
11510: \det\!\bigl(I-A_N(s)\bigr)\ \longrightarrow\ \det\!\bigl(I-A(s)\bigr),
11511: \qquad \text{uniformly for }s\in K.
11512: \]
11513: \end{theorem}
11514: 
11515: \begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
11516: This is a standard estimate in trace-ideal theory: the map $A\mapsto \det(I-A)$ is continuous in trace norm on bounded subsets of $\mathcal{T}_1$.
11517: One convenient inequality is
11518: \[
11519: \bigl|\det(I-A)-\det(I-B)\bigr|
11520: \le \|A-B\|_1\,\exp\!\bigl(1+\|A\|_1+\|B\|_1\bigr),
11521: \]
11522: valid for trace-class $A,B$.
11523: See, e.g., Simon's trace-ideal monograph \cite{SimonTraceIdeals}.
11524: \end{proof}
11525: 
11526: \subsection{Determinantal HP--GRH theorem: a complete conditional proof}
11527: \label{sec:determinantal_HP_GRH_theorem}
11528: 
11529: We can now state the exact ``last theorem'' used to close the Hilbert--Pólya $\Rightarrow$ GRH argument in this manuscript.
11530: 
11531: \begin{theorem}[Determinantal Hilbert--Pólya closure for GRH (conditional, but fully spelled out)]
11532: \label{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH}
11533: Fix a completed $L$--function $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ holomorphic in $0<\Re(s)<1$.
11534: Assume that for each cutoff level $N$ one can construct:
11535: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
11536: \item a finite-dimensional reversible totally positive transfer matrix $T_{N,\varpi}(s)$ realized by a positive wiring diagram built from the toric degeneration of surface-order determinantal semi-invariants;
11537: \item a corresponding self-adjoint Hilbert--Pólya matrix $H_{N,\varpi}$ obtained from $T_{N,\varpi}$ by symmetrization and modular-Hamiltonian extraction (Corollary~\ref{cor:cycle_reversal_self_adjoint});
11538: \item an operator-valued holomorphic family $A_{N,\varpi}(s)$ (finite rank, trace class) whose Fredholm determinant agrees with the Hilbert--Pólya approximant:
11539: \[
11540: F_N(s;\varpi)=\exp(\alpha_{N,\varpi}+\beta_{N,\varpi}s)\,
11541: \det\!\Bigl(sI_{d_N}-\bigl(\tfrac12 I_{d_N}+ i H_{N,\varpi}\bigr)\Bigr)
11542: = \det\!\bigl(I-A_{N,\varpi}(s)\bigr);
11543: \]
11544: \item a limiting trace-class family $A_\varpi(s)$ such that $A_{N,\varpi}(s)\to A_\varpi(s)$ in trace norm uniformly on compacta in $0<\Re(s)<1$, and
11545: \[
11546: \Lambda(s,\varpi)=\det\!\bigl(I-A_\varpi(s)\bigr)
11547: \quad\text{in }0<\Re(s)<1
11548: \]
11549: (up to an exponential renormalization factor that does not create zeros).
11550: \end{enumerate}
11551: Then GRH holds for $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$.
11552: 
11553: Moreover, if the convergence in (d) is uniform in $\varpi$ on compact subsets of a parameter family, then the conclusion holds uniformly in that family (a GRH-strength statement).
11554: \end{theorem}
11555: 
11556: \begin{proof}
11557: By (a)--(b), each approximant $F_N(s;\varpi)$ is of Hilbert--Pólya form, hence all its zeros lie on $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:zeros_on_critical_line_finite}).
11558: By (c)--(d) and Theorem~\ref{thm:fredholm_det_continuity}, the functions $F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)$ converge to $\Lambda(\,\cdot\,,\varpi)$ uniformly on compacta in $0<\Re(s)<1$.
11559: Therefore Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh} applies and forces all nontrivial zeros of $\Lambda$ to lie on the critical line.
11560: 
11561: Family-uniformity is exactly Proposition~\ref{prop:family_hurwitz_grh}.
11562: \end{proof}
11563: 
11564: 
11565: \begin{theorem}[Determinantal Hilbert--P\'olya $\Rightarrow$ (Artin holomorphy \emph{and} GRH) from a single convergence statement]
11566: \label{thm:hp_convergence_implies_holomorphy_and_grh}
11567: Let $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ be a completed $L$--function for which the Dirichlet series/Euler product converges absolutely in $\Re(s)>1$
11568: (hence $\Lambda$ is holomorphic there), and assume $\Lambda$ is known \emph{a priori} to admit meromorphic continuation to $\C$
11569: (e.g.\ by Artin formalism/Brauer induction in the Artin case).
11570: 
11571: Assume that one constructs a sequence of Hilbert--P\'olya determinantal approximants $F_N(s;\varpi)$ with the following properties:
11572: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11573: \item (\textbf{HP form / zero location}) Each $F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)$ has all zeros on the critical line $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$
11574: (e.g.\ $F_N(s;\varpi)=\det(sI-(\tfrac12 I+iH_{N,\varpi}))$ with $H_{N,\varpi}$ self-adjoint, as in Section~\ref{sec:determinantal_HP_GRH_theorem}).
11575: \item (\textbf{Holomorphy of approximants}) Each $F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)$ is holomorphic on the strip $0<\Re(s)<1$.
11576: \item (\textbf{Uniform convergence}) $F_N(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)\to F(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)$ uniformly on compact subsets of $0<\Re(s)<1$.
11577: \item (\textbf{Matching on an overlap}) There exists a nonempty open set $U\subset\{\Re(s)>1\}$ such that
11578: \[
11579: F(s;\varpi)=\Lambda(s,\varpi)\qquad \text{for all }s\in U.
11580: \]
11581: \end{enumerate}
11582: Then:
11583: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
11584: \item $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ is holomorphic on $0<\Re(s)<1$ (in particular, Artin holomorphy holds in the critical strip for the given $\varpi$); and
11585: \item all nontrivial zeros of $\Lambda(s,\varpi)$ in $0<\Re(s)<1$ lie on $\Re(s)=\tfrac12$ (GRH for $\Lambda$).
11586: \end{enumerate}
11587: \end{theorem}
11588: 
11589: \begin{proof}
11590: By (ii)--(iii), $F(\,\cdot\,;\varpi)$ is holomorphic on $0<\Re(s)<1$ by the Weierstrass theorem (Proposition~\ref{prop:weierstrass_holomorphy_from_approximation}).
11591: By (iv), $F$ agrees with $\Lambda$ on a nonempty open subset of the domain where $\Lambda$ is holomorphic; by the identity theorem, $F$ is the analytic continuation of $\Lambda$ to $0<\Re(s)<1$.
11592: Since $F$ is holomorphic on the whole strip, $\Lambda$ has no poles there, proving (a).
11593: 
11594: For (b), combine (i) with (iii) and apply Hurwitz' theorem in the form stated as Theorem~\ref{thm:hurwitz_hp_implies_grh}.
11595: \end{proof}
11596: 
11597: \begin{remark}[What this theorem does (and does not) solve]
11598: Theorem~\ref{thm:hp_convergence_implies_holomorphy_and_grh} is \emph{unconditional as a logical implication}:
11599: it reduces holomorphy and GRH to the construction of a determinantal HP approximation that matches $\Lambda$ on an overlap.
11600: What is hard (and where all arithmetic content lives) is establishing the existence of such a sequence and proving (iii)--(iv).
11601: The purpose of the surface-order/dessin and invariant-theory machinery earlier in the manuscript is to make (iv) a \emph{geometric matching problem in determinantal coordinates} rather than an ad hoc analytic miracle.
11602: \end{remark}
11603: 
11604: 
11605: 
11606: 
11607: \begin{remark}[Where the determinantal input from Schreiber/CCKW enters]
11608: Theorem~\ref{thm:determinantal_HP_GRH} is intentionally ``modular'':
11609: it separates what is \emph{representation theory/invariant theory} (toric degeneration, determinantal semi-invariants, Cartan determinant cycle factorizations) from what is \emph{analysis} (trace-class limits and determinant continuity) and what is \emph{operator algebra} (total positivity $\Rightarrow$ self-adjoint modular generators).
11610: The Artin/surface-order package (Theorem~\ref{thm:artin_determinantal_toric_package}) is precisely the input ensuring that the arithmetic Euler factors can be encoded by determinantal invariants that survive base change and behave functorially in $\varpi$.
11611: \end{remark}
11612: 
11613: \begin{remark}[Why weak-$\ast$ convergence is the right minimal target]
11614: Even when trace-norm convergence is analytically hard, weak-$\ast$ convergence of the normalized states $\rho_{N,\varpi}$ is a \emph{physically meaningful} minimal target:
11615: it is exactly the convergence notion preserved by CPTP coarse-graining channels.
11616: Once weak-$\ast$ convergence is established, the remaining problem is to upgrade it to the trace-class control required by Theorem~\ref{thm:fredholm_det_continuity}.
11617: This upgrade is where renormalization, decay of cycle weights, and toric positivity are expected to do real work.
11618: \end{remark}
11619: 
11620: %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
11621: 
11622: 
11623: 
11624: %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
11625: 
11626: \subsection{Geometric traits of $\mathrm{SL}$ semi-invariants and $\mathrm{GL}$ rational invariants, and why this helps with holomorphy}
11627: \label{sec:CC_SL_GL_geometry_holomorphy}
11628: 
11629: We briefly isolate the geometric input from invariant theory that is used implicitly in the determinantal holomorphy story.
11630: The guiding philosophy is:
11631: 
11632: \medskip
11633: \centerline{\fbox{\parbox{0.92\textwidth}{
11634: \emph{Determinants produce algebraic coordinates; geometry tells you what denominators are allowed; analytic continuation becomes a question about which potential poles are actually realized.}
11635: }}}
11636: \medskip
11637: 
11638: \paragraph{Setup: representation varieties and base change groups.}
11639: Let $A=kQ/I$ be a bound quiver algebra and fix a dimension vector $\mathbf{d}$.
11640: The affine variety $\Rep(A,\mathbf{d})$ carries the base-change action of
11641: \[
11642: G_{\mathbf{d}}=\prod_{i\in Q_0}\GL_{d_i}(k),
11643: \qquad
11644: \SL_{\mathbf{d}}=\prod_i \SL_{d_i}(k)\subset G_{\mathbf{d}}.
11645: \]
11646: Polynomial $\SL_{\mathbf{d}}$-invariants are precisely the \emph{semi-invariants} used in GIT constructions of moduli spaces (Section~\ref{sec:schofield_determinants}).
11647: 
11648: \paragraph{$\mathrm{GL}$-invariants versus $\mathrm{SL}$-semi-invariants.}
11649: For a quiver without oriented cycles, polynomial $G_{\mathbf{d}}$-invariants are trivial; in the general case, Le Bruyn--Procesi identify them as the trace algebra generated by traces along oriented cycles \cite{LeBruynProcesi1990SemisimpleQuivers}.
11650: Either way, the \emph{geometrically useful} invariants for moduli are the $\SL_{\mathbf{d}}$-semi-invariants: they cut out stability conditions and generate the homogeneous coordinate rings of GIT quotients.
11651: 
11652: 
11653: \subsubsection{Necklaces, cycle traces, and the Le Bruyn--Procesi--Lusztig generators}
11654: \label{sec:necklaces_LBP_Lusztig}
11655: 
11656: Because later sections translate the dessin/surface cycle calculus into invariant-theoretic coordinates, it is useful to isolate the precise ``cycle-generation'' statements.
11657: 
11658: \paragraph{Necklaces.}
11659: Fix a quiver $Q=(Q_0,Q_1)$ and a dimension vector $\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_0}$.
11660: Write
11661: \[
11662: \Rep(Q,\mathbf{d})=\bigoplus_{a\in Q_1} \Mat_{d_{h(a)}\times d_{t(a)}}(k)
11663: \]
11664: with base-change group $G_{\mathbf{d}}=\prod_{i\in Q_0}\GL_{d_i}(k)$ acting by simultaneous change of bases.
11665: For a nontrivial oriented cycle
11666: \(\gamma=a_\ell\cdots a_1\) in $Q$, define the \emph{necklace trace function}
11667: \[
11668: \tr_{\gamma} : \Rep(Q,\mathbf{d})\to k,\qquad
11669: \tr_{\gamma}(V):=\mathrm{tr}\bigl(V(a_\ell)\cdots V(a_1)\bigr).
11670: \]
11671: The terminology ``necklace'' reflects that $\tr_{\gamma}$ depends only on the cyclic word class of $\gamma$ (cyclic rotation does not change the trace).
11672: 
11673: \paragraph{Le Bruyn--Procesi.}
11674: The classical theorem of Le Bruyn and Procesi states that the invariant ring
11675: \(k[\Rep(Q,\mathbf{d})]^{G_{\mathbf{d}}}\)
11676: is generated, as a $k$-algebra, by the necklace trace functions $\{\tr_{\gamma}\}$ over all oriented cycles $\gamma$ in $Q$.
11677: Equivalently, all polynomial invariants for simultaneous base-change are ``Wilson loop'' traces. \cite{LeBruynProcesi1990SemisimpleQuivers}
11678: 
11679: \paragraph{Procesi's trace philosophy.}
11680: At the level of matrices, Procesi showed that invariants of tuples of matrices under simultaneous conjugation are generated by traces of words, subject to universal trace identities. \cite{Procesi1976InvariantTheoryMatrices}
11681: Le Bruyn--Procesi reduces the general quiver case to this matrix invariant theory by encoding quiver representations as structured matrix tuples.
11682: 
11683: \paragraph{Quivers with relations: Craw--Yamagishi (``Le Bruyn--Procesi following Lusztig'').}
11684: In practice one works with a \emph{quiver algebra} $A=kQ/J$ and the representation subscheme $\Rep(A,\mathbf{d})\subseteq \Rep(Q,\mathbf{d})$ cut out by the ideal of relations $J$.
11685: Restriction of functions gives a surjective homomorphism
11686: \(
11687: \tau: k[\Rep(Q,\mathbf{d})]^{G_{\mathbf{d}}}\twoheadrightarrow k[\Rep(A,\mathbf{d})]^{G_{\mathbf{d}}}.
11688: \)
11689: Craw--Yamagishi refine Le Bruyn--Procesi by identifying generators \emph{and} relations for the quotient: the target ring is generated by trace functions of cycles, and the kernel of $\tau$ is generated by trace functions coming from cycles lying in the vertex-idempotent corners of $\ker(kQ\twoheadrightarrow A)$ (Theorem~1.1 in \cite{CrawYamagishi2023LBPFollowingLusztig}). \cite{CrawYamagishi2023LBPFollowingLusztig}
11690: This is exactly the ``ideal kills certain necklaces'' principle used throughout the surface/dessin discussion.
11691: 
11692: \paragraph{Frozen vertices and open necklaces: Lusztig's generators.}
11693: Lusztig generalized the Le Bruyn--Procesi theorem to situations where the group acts only on a subset of vertices (``frozen'' vertices in modern cluster terminology).
11694: In Craw--Yamagishi's formulation, for a subset $K\subseteq Q_0$ one considers the subgroup
11695: \(G_K:=\prod_{k\in K}\GL_{d_k}(k)\subseteq G_{\mathbf{d}}.\)
11696: Then the invariant ring $k[\Rep(Q,\mathbf{d})]^{G_K}$ is generated by:
11697: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
11698: \item necklace traces $\tr_{\gamma}$ for cycles $\gamma$ whose arrows stay in the full subquiver on $K$; and
11699: \item \emph{contraction functions} $x_{p,ij}$ given by matrix entries of path products along paths $p$ with head/tail in the unfrozen complement $K^c$.
11700: \end{enumerate}
11701: See Theorem~2.2 in \cite{CrawYamagishi2023LBPFollowingLusztig}. \cite{CrawYamagishi2023LBPFollowingLusztig}
11702: From the ``necklace'' viewpoint, the functions $x_{p,ij}$ are \emph{open necklaces} (paths with framed endpoints), and determinants of matrices of such contractions recover the familiar determinantal semi-invariants after imposing $\SL$-conditions (compare Schofield/Derksen--Weyman and Shmelkin's bidirected-walk generators).
11703: 
11704: \begin{remark}[Relation to the surface/dessin cycle picture]
11705: In the dessin/surface-algebra regime, the relevant cycles are controlled by the permutations $(\sigma_0,\sigma_1,\sigma_\infty)$.
11706: Necklace traces attached to $\sigma_0$- and $\sigma_1$-cycles provide $G_{\mathbf d}$-invariant ``Wilson loop'' observables, while the relations in the ideal (killing the $\sigma_\infty$-cycles) correspond to declaring certain necklaces null.
11707: The adjacent-cycle reversal move discussed earlier can be interpreted as replacing one necklace representative by another in the same trace class while changing the associated shift/cycle weight; in the toric limit this becomes a binomial relation in the semigroup algebra.
11708: \end{remark}
11709: 
11710: \paragraph{Why cycle-generation matters for the determinantal holomorphy program.}
11711: The determinantal approximation scheme in Sections~\ref{sec:holomorphy_from_determinants}--\ref{sec:general_HP_construction} ultimately manipulates Euler factors of the form
11712: \(\det(I-\rho(\Frob_p)p^{-s})\), and hence trace polynomials in the matrices $\rho(\Frob_p)$.
11713: Newton identities express coefficients of characteristic polynomials as polynomials in the power-sum traces $\mathrm{tr}(\rho(\Frob_p)^m)$.
11714: Le Bruyn--Procesi--Lusztig therefore justify the methodological reduction:
11715: \begin{quote}
11716: \emph{to control the algebraic part of the global analytic continuation problem it suffices to control (closed or open) necklace traces and their determinantal composites.}
11717: \end{quote}
11718: In particular, ``geometric control of denominators'' in rational invariant coordinates can be phrased entirely in the language of cycles (necklaces) and their determinantal refinements.
11719: 
11720: \begin{lemma}[Newton identities, determinants, and necklace traces]
11721: \label{lem:newton_necklace}
11722: Let $R$ be a commutative $\Q$-algebra and let $X\in \Mat_{n\times n}(R)$.
11723: Write
11724: \[
11725: \det(\lambda I - X)=\lambda^n + c_1(X)\lambda^{n-1}+\cdots + c_n(X),
11726: \qquad c_m(X)=(-1)^m e_m(X),
11727: \]
11728: where $e_m(X)$ is the $m$-th elementary symmetric polynomial in the eigenvalues of $X$ (equivalently, the coefficient functions of the characteristic polynomial).
11729: For $m\ge 1$ define the \emph{power-sum traces} $p_m(X):=\tr(X^m)\in R$.
11730: 
11731: Then:
11732: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11733: \item \textbf{Newton identities.} For $1\le m\le n$ one has the recursive relations
11734: \[
11735: m\,e_m(X)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{i-1}e_{m-i}(X)\,p_i(X),
11736: \qquad e_0(X)=1.
11737: \]
11738: In particular, each $c_m(X)$ is a polynomial with rational coefficients in $p_1(X),\dots,p_m(X)$, and conversely each $p_m(X)$ is a polynomial in $c_1(X),\dots,c_m(X)$.
11739: \item \textbf{Log--det generating function.} As a formal power series in an indeterminate $t$,
11740: \[
11741: \det(I-tX)=\exp\!\Bigl(-\sum_{m\ge 1}\frac{t^m}{m}\,p_m(X)\Bigr).
11742: \]
11743: \end{enumerate}
11744: \end{lemma}
11745: 
11746: \begin{proof}
11747: Work in the ring $R[[t]]$.
11748: Differentiate $\log\det(I-tX)$ using $\frac{d}{dt}\log\det A(t)=\tr(A(t)^{-1}A'(t))$:
11749: \[
11750: \frac{d}{dt}\log\det(I-tX)
11751: =\tr\!\bigl((I-tX)^{-1}(-X)\bigr)
11752: =-\tr\!\bigl(X(I-tX)^{-1}\bigr).
11753: \]
11754: Expand $(I-tX)^{-1}=\sum_{m\ge 0} t^m X^m$ to obtain
11755: \[
11756: \frac{d}{dt}\log\det(I-tX)=-\sum_{m\ge 0} t^m\,\tr(X^{m+1})
11757: =-\sum_{m\ge 1} t^{m-1}\,p_m(X).
11758: \]
11759: Integrating termwise (and using $\det(I)=1$) yields the log--det identity.
11760: Writing $\det(I-tX)=\sum_{m=0}^n (-1)^m e_m(X)t^m$ and comparing coefficients after differentiating both sides recovers the Newton recursion.
11761: \end{proof}
11762: 
11763: \begin{corollary}[Euler factors are polynomials in necklace traces]
11764: \label{cor:euler_factors_necklaces}
11765: Let $\gamma$ be an oriented cycle in a quiver (or in a quiver algebra $A=kQ/J$) and let $V$ be a representation so that $X:=V(\gamma)$ is a square matrix.
11766: Then the coefficients of $\det(I-tX)$ are polynomials in the traces $\tr(X^m)$.
11767: Moreover $\tr(X^m)=\tr_{\gamma^m}(V)$ is itself a \emph{necklace trace} attached to the cycle word $\gamma^m$.
11768: Consequently:
11769: \begin{quote}
11770: \emph{every characteristic-polynomial/Euler-factor expression built from cycle monodromies is generated by necklace trace functions (and in the presence of frozen vertices, by Lusztig's open-necklace contractions).}
11771: \end{quote}
11772: \end{corollary}
11773: 
11774: \begin{proof}
11775: The first statement is Lemma~\ref{lem:newton_necklace}.
11776: The identity $\tr(X^m)=\tr_{\gamma^m}(V)$ is immediate from the definition of $\tr_{\gamma}$.
11777: Cycle-generation for $G_{\mathbf d}$-invariants and the ``ideal kills necklaces'' mechanism for quivers with relations were recorded above (Le Bruyn--Procesi and Craw--Yamagishi/Lusztig).
11778: \end{proof}
11779: 
11780: \paragraph{Where this closes the \emph{algebraic} side of the determinantal approximation scheme.}
11781: Sections~\ref{sec:schofield_determinants}--\ref{sec:holomorphy_from_determinants} use determinants in two logically distinct ways:
11782: \begin{enumerate}[label=(A\arabic*)]
11783: \item \emph{Local Euler determinants} such as $\det(I-\rho(\Frob_p)p^{-s})$ and their Dirichlet-series logarithms;
11784: \item \emph{Geometric/GIT determinants} (Schofield/Derksen--Weyman and Shmelkin) producing semi-invariant coordinate rings and moduli.
11785: \end{enumerate}
11786: Lemma~\ref{lem:newton_necklace} and Corollary~\ref{cor:euler_factors_necklaces} show that \textbf{(A1)} reduces to necklace traces (closed cycles), while the Le Bruyn--Procesi--Lusztig generator theorem shows that \textbf{(A2)} reduces to necklace traces plus open-necklace contractions (paths with framed endpoints), whose determinants recover the classical determinantal semi-invariants.
11787: Therefore, once one has:
11788: \begin{itemize}
11789: \item a cycle/necklace description of the relevant invariant fields (CC, Shmelkin, and the surface/dessin cycle calculus), and
11790: \item analytic control of convergence/trace ideals for the resulting determinant sequence (Sections~\ref{sec:determinantal_invariants_gentle_toric}--\ref{sec:general_HP_construction}),
11791: \end{itemize}
11792: there is \emph{no additional algebraic generator problem}: all objects entering the determinant approximation can be rewritten in cycle language.
11793: 
11794: 
11795: 
11796: \paragraph{Carroll--Chindri\c{s}: rationality and projective-space moduli for acyclic gentle algebras.}
11797: For \emph{acyclic gentle algebras}, Carroll and Chindri\c{s} prove two key facts \cite{CarrollChindris2012InvariantGentle}:
11798: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
11799: \item On each irreducible component $C\subset \Rep(A,\mathbf{d})$, the field of rational invariants $k(C)^{G_{\mathbf{d}}}$ is a \emph{purely transcendental} extension of $k$.
11800: Moreover, they exhibit a transcendence basis in terms of \emph{Schofield determinantal semi-invariants} (ratios of determinants coming from projective presentations).
11801: \item The moduli spaces of $\theta$-semistable modules over \emph{regular} components are products of projective spaces.
